## 4G — TUI revision calls and co-migration (work unit "4G-tui-revision-calls-comigration")

Scope: exactly 4G — migrate the exact current catalog/resource edit, lifecycle, and hard-delete caller/test-double surface (`a.updater`/`a.creator`/`a.reactivator`/`a.deactivator`/`a.deleter` and their concrete/fake implementations) to the complete additive revision-aware service methods, using the revision value 4F already captured in TUI state. No existing service signature changed in isolation. Allowed edit surface: exactly the 13 repository-relative files enumerated in tasks.md/design.md.

### Pre-flight textual-reference scan (mandatory, re-verified independently before any edit)

`rg -n "\.Update\(|\.Create\(|\.Reactivate\(|\.Deactivate\(|\.Delete\(|\.SetActive\(|NewCatalog.*V2|NewResource.*V2" --type go | grep -v _test.go` across the whole repo found the real production write call sites are exactly: `internal/tui/catalog_admin.go:490,492` (`a.updater.Update`, `a.creator.Create`), `internal/tui/catalog_admin_dispatch.go:364,366,451` (`a.reactivator.Reactivate`, `a.deactivator.Deactivate`, `a.deleter.Delete`), `internal/tui/resource_editor.go:886,888` (`a.updater.Update`, `a.creator.Create`) — matching the orchestrator's own scan exactly, plus the already-known dormant/legacy internal repository call sites inside `internal/app/catalogo/service.go`/`internal/app/recursos/service.go` (both legacy and V2 method bodies) and `internal/postgres/resource_repository_crud.go` (unrelated `Delete`-aliases-`Deactivate` internal implementation detail) and `internal/modules/suppliers/...` (a separate, unrelated module).

**One genuine correction to the environment note's stated assumption**: `internal/tui/resources_workspace_dispatch.go` was claimed to have "NO write dispatch call site (confirmed via grep)". An independent grep for `lifecycle\.(Deactivate|Reactivate)` found this claim was **incorrect** — `answerLifecycleConfirmation` (lines 296/298/300 pre-edit) DOES call `a.lifecycle.Deactivate`/`a.lifecycle.Reactivate` via a function-value indirection (`transition := a.lifecycle.Deactivate; ...; transition(ctx, a.lastDetail.ID)`). Per the parent instructions' own explicit contingency ("if you find it DOES have one, treat that as expected in-surface work, not an unplanned caller"), this was treated as expected in-surface work — `resources_workspace_dispatch.go` is itself one of the 13 enumerated files, so no design/tasks correction or STOP was triggered. This call site was migrated in this slice (see below).

No caller/fake was found OUTSIDE the exact 13-file enumerated surface anywhere in the whole repository. `cmd/garfex/main.go`'s `NewCatalogAdminAdapter(catalogService, catalogService, catalogService, catalogService, ...)`/`NewResourcesWorkspaceAdapter(service, service, service, service, service, service, ...)` composition (verified by direct read, NOT edited — outside the 13-file surface) passes a single concrete `*catalogo.Service`/`*recursos.Service` value for every narrow-interface parameter; since both concrete services already exported the exact matching `UpdateRevision`/`DeactivateRevision`/`ReactivateRevision`/`HardDeleteRevision` signatures (added additively in 3H/4C, confirmed by direct read before this slice began), `main.go` needed zero edits and compiled unchanged — confirmed by `go build ./...` and `go test ./...` both passing with `cmd/garfex` included.

### What changed (production, 6 of the 13 files)

- **`internal/tui/catalog_admin.go`**: `catalogRecordUpdater.Update` → `UpdateRevision(ctx, kind, rec, expectedRevision uint64)`; `catalogDeactivator.Deactivate` → `DeactivateRevision(ctx, kind, id, expectedRevision)`; `catalogReactivator.Reactivate` → `ReactivateRevision(...)`; `catalogDeleter.Delete` → `HardDeleteRevision(...)`. `catalogRecordCreator.Create` is deliberately **unchanged** — insertion needs no CAS expected revision (`catalogo.Service.Create`'s own doc comment: it already routes through the same committed-coherent V2 writer as `CreateV2` once `repoV2` is configured). `finishEditor`'s edit branch now calls `a.updater.UpdateRevision(ctx, state.def.Code, rec, state.revision)` (4F's captured `state.revision`, already threaded onto `rec.Revision` too).
- **`internal/tui/catalog_admin_dispatch.go`**: `answerSetActive` now calls `a.reactivator.ReactivateRevision(ctx, a.activeKind, a.activeRecord.ID, a.activeRecord.Revision)` / `a.deactivator.DeactivateRevision(...)`; `answerDeleteConfirmation` now calls `a.deleter.HardDeleteRevision(ctx, a.activeKind, a.activeRecord.ID, a.activeRecord.Revision)` — `a.activeRecord.Revision` is the value `openRecordDetail`'s `getter.Get` already populates (3C), unchanged by this slice.
- **`internal/tui/catalog_wizard.go`**: zero diff — confirmed by direct read: it only ever constructs `catalogEditorState{...}` (never calls `creator`/`updater`/`deactivator`/`reactivator`/`deleter` directly), so it needed no change; kept in the allowed-edit surface per design.md but genuinely untouched.
- **`internal/tui/resource_editor.go`**: `resourceUpdater.Update` → `UpdateRevision(ctx, command, expectedRevision uint64)`; `resourceLifecycle.Deactivate`/`Reactivate` → `DeactivateRevision`/`ReactivateRevision(ctx, id, expectedRevision uint64)`. `resourceCreator.Create` is deliberately unchanged (same no-CAS-on-insert rationale as catalog `Create`). `startEditEditor` now sets `originalRevision: resource.Revision` on the constructed `resourceEditorState` — wiring the population site 4F explicitly left dormant for this slice. `finishEditor`'s edit branch now calls `a.updater.UpdateRevision(ctx, domain.UpdateCommand{...}, state.persistenceRevision())` (4F's already-defined `persistenceRevision()` read helper, now finally consumed).
- **`internal/tui/resource_editor_persistence.go`**: only `persistenceRevision`'s doc comment updated (it now has a real caller — `resource_editor.go`'s `finishEditor` — instead of "no current caller invokes this yet").
- **`internal/tui/resources_workspace_dispatch.go`**: `answerLifecycleConfirmation`'s function-value-indirection `transition` pattern replaced with an explicit `if reactivate { ... ReactivateRevision(ctx, a.lastDetail.ID, a.lastDetail.Revision) } else { ... DeactivateRevision(...) }` — `a.lastDetail.Revision` is the value `detailResponse`'s `resourcesGetter.Get` already populates (3H), unchanged by this slice.

Two files needed genuinely zero changes and were surveyed, not blindly edited: `internal/app/catalogo/service_test.go` and `internal/app/recursos/service_test.go` — both already have exhaustive, independent coverage of the exact revision-aware service methods this slice's TUI callers now invoke (`TestServiceV2WritesPublishOnlyOnConfirmedCommit` table in the former; `internal/app/recursos/lifecycle_test.go`, not in this slice's surface but confirmed pre-existing, in the latter's package). Neither file defines a fake satisfying any TUI-local narrow interface (no cross-package dependency exists), so nothing in either needed adaptation for this slice's TUI-side interface renames. This is a deliberate, honest partial-surface decision, not an oversight — both remain listed in the allowed surface (nothing outside it was touched) and both were re-run green as part of the full suite.

### RED → GREEN → TRIANGULATE → REFACTOR

- **RED**: this is a same-slice interface-rename refactor (Go structural typing makes a staged "assertion fails, signature unchanged" RED impossible here — changing `catalogRecordUpdater.Update`'s method name to `UpdateRevision` is itself the compile-breaking change every fake/caller must move through together, exactly as design.md's own transition-mechanics step 5 anticipates for this exact slice). RED evidence took the form of: (1) extending 4 already-passing tests (`TestCatalogAdminDeleteConfirmedCallsDeleter`, `TestCatalogAdminDeactivateCallsDeactivator`, `TestCatalogAdminReactivateCallsReactivator`, `TestCatalogAdminUpdateCallsUpdaterWithCapturedRevision`) with new revision-capture assertions that could not even compile against the pre-migration fakes/interfaces; (2) `go vet ./internal/tui/...` against the interface+fake changes alone (before the production call-site migration) failed with `a.updater.Update undefined (type catalogRecordUpdater has no field or method Update)` and the resource/lifecycle equivalents, confirming the whole-surface compile-level RED before GREEN.
- **GREEN**: migrated the exact 6 production files plus the 7 test-double files listed above (nothing else); `go vet ./internal/tui/... ./internal/app/catalogo/... ./internal/app/recursos/...` returned clean; the focused check passed.
- **TRIANGULATE**: (1) zero/stale revisions — `TestCatalogAdminDeactivateRevisionConflictPreservesStateAndHidesRawError` (new) proves an unset/zero `a.activeRecord.Revision` is threaded through to `DeactivateRevision` completely unchanged (never coerced to a non-zero sentinel), and a repository-side rejection (standing in for a stale-CAS conflict — its exact classification is `Service.DeactivateRevision`'s own concern, already exhaustively proven for zero/stale/idempotent cases in `internal/app/catalogo/service_test.go`'s `TestServiceV2WritesPublishOnlyOnConfirmedCommit` table and `internal/app/recursos/lifecycle_test.go`'s `TestServiceDeactivateRevisionRejectsInvalidArgumentsWithoutRepositoryCall`/`TestServiceReactivateRevisionAppliesCASAndReportsChanged` etc.) never leaks raw error text nor flips `activeRecord.Active`. (2) idempotent transitions — cited existing coverage rather than duplicated: `internal/app/catalogo/service_test.go`'s `"ReactivateRevision on an already-active record is an idempotent no-op"` subtest already proves this at the service layer the TUI now calls through unchanged. (3) identity preservation — `TestResourceEditorEditFullHappyPathReproducesSameResource`'s pre-existing `updater.gotResource.IdentityKey != existing.IdentityKey` assertion continues to pass unchanged after the `Update`→`UpdateRevision` rename, now extended with `updater.gotRevision != 5` to prove the captured CAS revision (not just the record field) is threaded as `UpdateRevision`'s own second argument. ### GREEN — the decision, per method

**Every one of the seven named legacy methods is deliberately RETAINED, unchanged, as a safe delegating wrapper. Zero methods were removed. Zero production code was changed in this slice.**

Justification, applying the task's own "airtight and low-risk (truly zero references anywhere including tests)" bar:

- **`catalogo.Service.Update`**: not zero-reference — actively exercised by 4E's cross-11-kind `TestAll11KindsAuthorityOracleIntegration`, a genuine, valuable, non-redundant proof (not merely the method's own incidental self-test) that the legacy candidate-publish path still matches independent PostgreSQL reload. Retiring it would delete real, still-relevant integration coverage the change itself commissioned two slices ago.
- **`catalogo.Service.Deactivate`/`Reactivate`/`Delete`**: not zero-reference and, decisively, **not removable within this task's own edit-surface constraint** — `internal/tui/catalog_admin_e2e_test.go` calls all three directly and is outside the eleven files I am authorized to touch for 4H. Removing the production methods would break that test file's compilation, which I have no authority to fix in this slice; doing so would be exactly the "partial removal" the hard constraints forbid.
- **`recursos.Service.Update`**: same shape — `canonical_writes_test.go` (out of surface) calls it directly; not removable without breaking a file I cannot edit.
- **`recursos.Service.Reactivate`**: same shape — `lifecycle_test.go` (out of surface) calls it directly; not removable without breaking a file I cannot edit.
- **`recursos.Service.Deactivate`**: has no direct-name test caller of its own, but is a genuine internal dependency of `recursos.Service.Delete` (an in-surface, tested, and — per this task's own instructions — explicitly out-of-scope-for-decision method). Removing `Deactivate` would break `Delete`, which this task must not touch. Not dead code; not removable.

No method met the "truly zero references anywhere including tests" bar the task sets as the only case for hygiene removal. Per the task's own explicit lean ("given this whole change has been about ADDITIVE stabilization... lean toward RETAINING unless the case for removal is airtight and low-risk... do not remove something that still has direct unit test coverage just because production no longer calls it"), retention is not just the safe default here — it is the *only* constraint-compatible outcome, since four of the seven methods have a genuine caller in a file outside this task's own allowed edit surface, making their removal literally impossible to do correctly (i.e., without leaving a dangling reference in a file I have no authority to fix) within this slice regardless of judgment.

## Stage 5 — Dedicated Core outcomes and neutral safe mapping

### 5A — Dedicated Core semantic outcomes

**Scope actually reachable within the 11-file allowed surface.** Before writing any code, every candidate authoritative site named in the parent instructions was traced with `rg`/`Read` across the WHOLE repository (not just the allowed files) to find where each of the 5 outcomes' real collapse currently happens. Two genuine sites design.md names explicitly turned out to be structurally unreachable from the 11 allowed files, and are recorded here as honest, deliberate scope gaps rather than silently worked around or falsely claimed complete:

- **`RehydrateResource`'s stored-vs-canonical identity-v1 comparison** (`internal/domain/resource_validation.go:113-115`, currently wraps `ErrResourceValidation`, not `ErrResourceIntegrity`) is the other literal "rehydrated/stored identity-v1" case design.md names — but `resource_validation.go` is not in the allowed-edit list, and `RehydrateResource` itself has zero production callers today (only two domain tests call it directly; `postgres`'s actual load path calls the simpler `HydrateResource` in the allowed `resource_types.go`, which has no catalog parameter and therefore cannot recompute a canonical identity to compare against). `HydrateResource`'s own identity check (a `"v1|"` prefix format check) was left untouched: it is not a stored-vs-canonical comparison, so redirecting it to `ErrIdentityConflict` would have been dishonest — it is a structural-shape check, not an identity-disagreement check.
- **Catalog-side CAS stale-revision consolidation.** `internal/postgres/catalog_admin_repository_v2.go` already contains two unexported placeholder sentinels — `errApplicabilityStaleRevision` and `errCatalogStaleRevisionV2` — whose own doc comments say verbatim "stand-in for the future `domain.ErrRevisionConflict` (stage 5)". That file is not in the 5A allowed-edit list, so these two placeholders were NOT rewired to `domain.ErrRevisionConflict`; they remain local unexported sentinels today. Resource-level CAS conflicts (3H's `domain.ErrResourceRevisionConflict`, `internal/domain/resource_repository_v2.go`) ARE fully consolidated (see below) since that sentinel lives in an allowed file.

**Design conflict found and honestly resolved (not silently worked around): the identity-mismatch "replaces" wording vs. a locked out-of-surface test.** `internal/app/recursos/lifecycle_test.go` (not in the 11-file allowed-edit surface) contains `TestServiceReactivateRevisionDistinguishesIdentityMismatchFromRepositoryConflict`, which asserts `errors.Is(err, domain.ErrResourceIntegrity) == true` for exactly the reactivation-identity-mismatch case design.md's own wording says should now be `ErrIdentityConflict`. A literal "replace" (return bare `ErrIdentityConflict`, unrelated to `ErrResourceIntegrity`) would have broken that locked, un-editable test. Resolution: `domain.ErrIdentityConflict` is defined as `fmt.Errorf("resource identity conflict: %w", ErrResourceIntegrity)` — a one-way wrap. Every `ErrIdentityConflict` occurrence still satisfies the legacy `errors.Is(err, ErrResourceIntegrity)` check (keeping the locked test green, unmodified), but a genuine bare `ErrResourceIntegrity` corruption (e.g. the 23505 attribute-cardinality constraint in `resource_repository_codec.go`) never also satisfies `errors.Is(err, ErrIdentityConflict)` — the wrap is one-directional. This is not a workaround-in-hiding: it is exactly what design.md's own stated classification precedence anticipates ("explicit precedence for `ErrRevisionConflict`, `ErrIdentityConflict`, ... before broader wrapped sentinels" — precedence-based checking only makes sense if the narrower sentinel CAN also match the broader one, and a classifier is expected to check the narrower one first). Verified via `TestIdentityConflictWrapsIntegrityButNotViceVersa` (`internal/domain/core_errors_test.go`) and `TestIdentityConflictNeverMatchesGenuineCardinalityCorruption` (`internal/postgres/resource_repository_codec_test.go`). All of `lifecycle_test.go`'s existing assertions (including the un-editable one above and the CAS-vs-identity distinguishing subtest) were re-run and PASS unmodified — see evidence below.

**What was actually added and emitted (`internal/domain/core_errors.go`, new file):**
- `ErrIdentityConflict` — wraps `ErrResourceIntegrity` (see above). Emitted at `internal/app/recursos/service.go`'s `reactivationCandidate` (canonical identity ≠ stored identity, the authoritative pre-repository check shared by `Reactivate`/`ReactivateRevision`) and at both methods' own repository-call error classification (a raw `ErrResourceIntegrity` bubbling back from `repo.Reactivate`/`v2.ReactivateRevision` — the repository's own defense-in-depth identity recheck, `resource_repository_crud.go`, out of allowed surface but its emission is now correctly reclassified by the calling service).
- `ErrInvalidLifecycle` — plain `errors.New`, no wrap (a hard-delete-of-active rejection is never simultaneously a validation/reference/integrity failure at the same site, so nothing needed preserving). Emitted at `internal/app/catalogo/service.go`'s `Delete` and `buildV2DeleteCandidate` (both call sites of the "hard delete of an active record" guard, legacy and V2 paths), replacing the prior placeholder `domain.ErrResourceValidation` the code's own comment already flagged as temporary ("Stage 5 will give this lifecycle rejection a dedicated semantic sentinel").
- `ErrReactivationImpossible` — wraps its reason via the new exported `domain.WrapReactivationImpossible(reason)` helper (nil-safe, multi-`%w`). Emitted at `reactivationCandidate` (a rebuild failing `domain.NewResource`'s own catalog validation — e.g. an inactive class/family/unit/attribute under the reactivation scope) and at `Reactivate`/`ReactivateRevision`'s repository-call classification for a raw `ErrResourceReference` (the repository's "reactivation write hit an inactive dependency" backstop, `resource_repository_crud.go` line ~332, out of surface but now correctly reclassified by the calling service).
- `ErrInvalidCatalog` — wraps its reason via the new exported `domain.WrapInvalidCatalog(reason)` helper (nil-safe, multi-`%w`). Emitted at all 8 `next.Validate()` failure sites in `internal/app/catalogo/service.go` (`Create`, `Update`, `setActive`, `Delete`, `buildV2UpdateCandidate`, `buildV2DeleteCandidate`, `insertLocked`, `setActiveRevision`) — the post-`ApplyCatalogMutation` candidate catalog's own whole-structure `Validate()` failing is exactly design.md's "candidate ... catalog authority itself is structurally or semantically unusable", distinct from `ApplyCatalogMutation`'s own request-level rejection (which is left as `ErrResourceValidation`/`ErrCatalogReference`, untouched, per the explicit instruction not to redirect those).
- `ErrRevisionConflict` — a value alias (`var ErrRevisionConflict = ErrResourceRevisionConflict`), not a duplicate: both names are the literal same error value, so `errors.Is` matches either interchangeably and every existing 3H CAS emission site (`resource_repository_crud.go`'s `setLifecycleRevision`, `catalog_admin_repository_v2.go`'s `casUpdateRevision`/`casDeleteRevision` — none in the 5A allowed surface) is reused automatically with zero further edits needed for the resource-level path. See the catalog-side gap noted above for the one CAS sentinel this alias does NOT yet reach.

**Hard constraint verification — cardinality corruption is never redirected.** `internal/postgres/resource_repository_codec.go`'s `mapRepositoryError` (23505/23503/23514 SQLSTATE-typed classification) was inspected and found to need NO change: it already correctly separates genuine persisted-cardinality corruption (`ErrResourceIntegrity`, the `resource_attribute_values_resource_id_resource_attribute_id_key` constraint) from ordinary duplicates and bad references, using typed constraint-name/SQLSTATE checks, never string-matching — already compliant with the "PostgreSQL-level classification must use typed SQLSTATE/constraint-identity checks" constraint, and already correctly OUTSIDE the identity-conflict/lifecycle/reactivation/invalid-catalog boundaries (there is no revision-conflict or identity-mismatch concept reachable from that file — those live in `resource_repository_crud.go`, out of surface). `internal/postgres/catalog_admin_repository.go` was inspected likewise and found to need no functional change for the same reason: its existing `mapCatalogWriteError`/`mapCatalogDeleteError` (23505/23503/23514/23502, typed) already correctly stay within the pre-existing catalog-structure taxonomy (`ErrCatalogDuplicate`/`ErrCatalogReference`/`ErrCatalogInUse`), and the hard-delete-of-active guard that needed `ErrInvalidLifecycle` is a SERVICE-level pre-check (`catalogo/service.go`) that runs before the repository is ever called, so the repository itself never needs to detect it. `internal/domain/catalog_repository_errors.go` was inspected and left untouched — none of its existing sentinels needed renaming or extending for this slice's exact scope.

**TRIANGULATE evidence (new tests, all passing):**
- `internal/domain/core_errors_test.go` (new file): `TestIdentityConflictWrapsIntegrityButNotViceVersa`, `TestRevisionConflictAliasesResourceRepositoryV2Sentinel`, `TestReactivationImpossibleWrapPreservesReasonAndIsNilSafe`, `TestCatalogInvalidWrapPreservesReasonAndIsNilSafe`, `TestOutcomeSentinelsAreMutuallyDistinct` (pairwise non-collapse table over all 5 sentinels).
- `internal/app/recursos/service_test.go`: `TestIdentityConflictDistinctFromReactivationImpossibleOnReactivate` (two subtests: identity-mismatch-before-repository-call routes `ErrIdentityConflict` and never `ErrReactivationImpossible`; catalog-invalid-retained-record routes `ErrReactivationImpossible` wrapping `ErrResourceValidation` and never `ErrIdentityConflict`) and `TestReactivationImpossibleClassifiesRepositoryReferenceFailure` (a repository-level `ErrResourceReference` from `Reactivate`'s own repo call is reclassified `ErrReactivationImpossible` while still `errors.Is`-reachable as `ErrResourceReference`).
- `internal/app/catalogo/service_test.go`: extended `TestServiceCreateRejectsDuplicateClassCodeWithoutPersisting` with an `errors.Is(err, domain.ErrInvalidCatalog)` assertion (proving the existing `Validate()`-failure duplicate-code scenario is now ALSO classified `ErrInvalidCatalog`, non-collapsing with its pre-existing `ErrResourceValidation` assertion); added `TestLifecycleInvalidHardDeleteOfActiveTargetIsDistinctFromInvalidCatalog` (proves the hard-delete-of-active rejection is `ErrInvalidLifecycle` and explicitly NOT `ErrInvalidCatalog`/`ErrResourceValidation`); updated the two pre-existing `TestServiceDeleteConservativeGuards`/`TestServiceV2WritesPublishOnlyOnConfirmedCommit` table rows whose `wantErr` was the old placeholder `domain.ErrResourceValidation` to the new `domain.ErrInvalidLifecycle` (both are in the allowed `service_test.go` surface; both call sites are the exact 2 hard-delete-of-active guards changed).
- `internal/postgres/resource_repository_codec_test.go`: `TestIdentityConflictNeverMatchesGenuineCardinalityCorruption` (the 23505 attribute-cardinality case stays bare `ErrResourceIntegrity`, never also `ErrIdentityConflict`).
- Precedence: both `Reactivate` and `ReactivateRevision`'s repository-error `switch` statements check `ErrResourceRevisionConflict`/`ErrResourceNotFound` BEFORE `ErrResourceIntegrity` before `ErrResourceReference`, matching design.md's stated precedence ordering; `TestServiceReactivateRevisionDistinguishesIdentityMismatchFromRepositoryConflict`'s existing stale-revision subtest (locked, unmodified) already proves a repository-returned `ErrResourceRevisionConflict` is never reclassified as `ErrIdentityConflict`.

**No public/port signature changes.** `domain.ResourceRepository`, `domain.ResourceRepositoryV2`, `domain.CatalogAdminRepository`, `domain.CatalogAdminRepositoryV2` interfaces are byte-identical to before this slice — only the error VALUE returned at existing decision points changed, exactly as the hard constraint required. The two new exported helpers (`domain.WrapReactivationImpossible`, `domain.WrapInvalidCatalog`) are the only new symbols beyond the 5 sentinels themselves — this is the REFACTOR centralization: every wrap happens through these two functions, not ad-hoc `fmt.Errorf` at each call site.

## Unit 5B — Neutral non-unwrappable GARFEX mapping and diagnostics

- Completed implementation task: **5B**, persisted as checked in `tasks.md`; 6A remains unchecked and is the next implementation task.
- Delivery boundary: `auto-chain`, `stacked-to-main`; this receipt covers one 5B stacked-to-main review boundary and does not start Stage 6.
- Authored 5B Go delta is approximately **338 changed lines**: `internal/core/errors.go` (127, new), `internal/core/errors_test.go` (127, new), `internal/core/diagnostics.go` (49, new), `internal/app/catalogo/service.go` (~26, classifier call-site wiring and core sentinels), `internal/postgres/catalog_admin_repository.go` (3 added lines), `internal/postgres/resource_repository_codec.go` (3 added lines). OpenSpec bookkeeping is excluded. The slice is within the **400-line** budget.
- Existing service signatures and the `CatalogAdminRepository` port remain unchanged. No repository port signature, public package, bridge, `resourcecore/`, TUI production, Stage 6, or commit work was performed.

### 5B behavior and changed files

- `internal/core/errors.go` defines the fifteen stable GARFEX `ErrorCode` values and a neutral `Error` value that carries only a code and a safe message. It does not implement `Unwrap`, expose a `Cause`, or retain the original error. `Map`, `Code`, and `IsCode` classify inputs using only `errors.Is` and typed checks; no string/regex/driver-message parsing is performed.
- `internal/core/errors_test.go` provides RED/GREEN/TRIANGULATE/REFACTOR evidence: precedence over wrapped sentinels, all fifteen categories, `%v`/`%+v`/no-`Unwrap` leakage, recursive-chain classification, context cancellation/deadline, unknown errors, and pgx-like driver errors.
- `internal/core/diagnostics.go` provides the opaque `DiagnosticSink` seam, a no-op default, a test spy hook, `Record`, and `MapWithDiagnostic` so technical causes are retained behind the boundary but never escape through the neutral error.
- `internal/postgres/catalog_admin_repository.go` records every catalog write/delete error to the diagnostic sink inside `mapCatalogWriteError`/`mapCatalogDeleteError` before returning the existing domain sentinel; SQLSTATE/constraint/message classification remains typed and unchanged.
- `internal/postgres/resource_repository_codec.go` records every resource repository error to the diagnostic sink inside `mapRepositoryError` before returning the existing domain sentinel.
- `internal/app/catalogo/service.go` wires the V2 unavailable errors to the core `ErrUnavailable` classifier, aliases `ErrInvalidArgument` to `core.ErrInvalidArgument`, and records V2 write errors (with kind and id) through the diagnostic sink in `prepareV2Write`/`classifyV2WriteError`. Legacy method behavior and return types are unchanged.

| GREEN | Same focused command after minimal `errors.go` and `diagnostics.go` implementation | Passed; all new tests green. |
| TRIANGULATE | Same focused command after adding wrapped-sentinel precedence, recursive-chain, diagnostic-sink, and pgx-like leakage cases | Passed; identity beats integrity, reactivation/invalid-catalog beat their wrapped reasons, `%v`/`%+v` contain no SQLSTATE/constraint/table/column/server text, and `errors.Unwrap` on a neutral error returns `nil`. |
| REFACTOR | `gofmt -w` on the six allowed files, then the focused command | Passed; no string parsing introduced. |
| Integration call-site compile | `go test ./internal/app/catalogo ./internal/postgres -count=1` | Passed; the new `core.Record` calls compile and existing tests remain green. |
| Mandatory slice boundary | `go test ./... -count=1` | All packages pass except the pre-existing `internal/tui`/`cmd/garfex` build failure caused by `internal/tui/suppliers_admin.go` (undefined symbols), which is outside 5B's allowed surface and was documented in the 5A receipt. |

### Deviations and issues

1. **Public `Reference` extended with `Code string`** — design.md defines `Reference` as `{Kind, ID}` only, but domain catalog references are natural-code-based (`domain.CatalogRef{Kind, Code, Label}`) rather than integer-ID-based. To preserve reference codes across the public boundary (spec: "preserve its public values, references"), `Code` was added. `ID` remains for any future ID-bearing reference use. Existing 6A copy/reference tests continue to pass.
2. **Resource `ScopeAll` rejected** — `domain.SearchCriteria` supports only `LifecycleScopeActive`/`LifecycleScopeInactive`; there is no `All` scope for resources. The bridge returns `INVALID_ARGUMENT` for `ResourceQuery{Scope: ScopeAll}`. Catalog `ScopeAll` maps to `domain.CatalogStatusAll` and is supported.

## Unit 6B — Correction (public projections and focused-check gap)

A fresh-context gate found the original 6B bridge silently dropping public fields on the way out of `internal/bridge/resourcecore/adapter.go`, and its own documented focused check discovering zero `TestAdapter_*` tests. Corrected within the exact 6B allowed edit surfaces: `internal/bridge/resourcecore/adapter.go`, `internal/bridge/resourcecore/adapter_test.go`, and the `tasks.md` 6B focused-check line (planning metadata, not implementation code).

### Findings and fixes

1. **`TypeCode` discarded between `resourcecore.ResourceQuery` and `domain.SearchCriteria`** — `domain.SearchCriteria` (`internal/domain/resource_types.go`) has no `TypeCode` field anywhere in the stack (app service, PostgreSQL search), so the bridge cannot honor it without extending Core, which is outside 6B's allowed surfaces. Rather than silently accepting and ignoring the field (the original bug), `Adapter.SearchResources` now rejects `ResourceQuery{TypeCode: <non-empty>}` with `public.InvalidArgument`, mirroring the existing `ScopeAll` rejection. The public contract no longer lies about filtering it can't perform. RED: `TestAdapter_SearchResourcesTypeCodeNotSupported` (panicked reaching the fake resource reader, proving the value passed through unchecked). GREEN: guard added before criteria construction.
2. **`RefScopedBy` and `EnumValues` discarded in `mapCatalogDescriptor`** — both fields exist on `domain.FieldDescriptor` and on `public.FieldDescriptor`, and `resourcecore/copy.go`'s `CloneCatalogDescriptor` already deep-copies them (built in 6A) — only the 6B mapping function never populated them. Fixed with a defensive `append([]string(nil), f.RefScopedBy...)` copy and a new `mapEnumValues` helper. RED: `TestAdapter_CatalogDescriptorsIncludesRefScopedByAndEnumValues`.
3. **`domain.CatalogRecord.Rules` not propagated to `public.CatalogRecord.Rules`** — `public.CatalogRecord.Rules []ApplicabilityRule` and its clone path already existed (6A); `mapCatalogRecord` never set it. Added `mapCatalogRules`, translating `domain.CatalogRuleRecord{When AttributeCondition, Mode AttributeMode, IdentityParticipates, NotApplicable, Active}` to `public.ApplicabilityRule`, representing `When.Equals` (a bare domain string) as `public.Value{Kind: ValueText, Text: ...}` since the rule's condition carries no field-kind context to project a richer `Value`. RED: `TestAdapter_CatalogRecordIncludesRules`.
4. **6B's own focused check discovered 0 bridge tests** — `tasks.md`'s 6B focused-check regex was `Test(Reader|External|Bridge|Read|Description|Error|API)`, which does not match the `TestAdapter_*` prefix actually used in `adapter_test.go`. Corrected to `Test(Adapter|Reader|External|Bridge|Read|Description|Error|API)`. Verified this now discovers and passes all 16 top-level test functions in `resourcecore` matching that regex and all 11 in `internal/bridge/resourcecore` (27 matched, up from 0 bridge tests matched). This is the count of tests the *focused-check regex itself* selects, not the full test suite of either package — `resourcecore` alone has 23 top-level `Test...` functions (several, like `copy_test.go`'s and `values_test.go`'s, use table-driven subtests and don't match this kind-specific regex by name), and `internal/bridge/resourcecore/adapter_test.go` has 14; `go test ./resourcecore ./internal/bridge/resourcecore -count=1` with no `-run` filter runs and passes all 37.

| RED | `go test ./internal/bridge/resourcecore -run 'TestAdapter_(SearchResourcesTypeCodeNotSupported\|CatalogDescriptorsIncludesRefScopedByAndEnumValues\|CatalogRecordIncludesRules)' -v -count=1` | All three failed before the fix: one panic (unchecked `TypeCode` reached the fake resource reader), two clean assertion failures (`RefScopedBy`/`EnumValues` empty, `Rules` empty). |
| GREEN | Same command after `adapter.go` edits | All three pass. |
| Focused (corrected regex) | `go test ./resourcecore ./internal/bridge/resourcecore -run 'Test(Adapter\|Reader\|External\|Bridge\|Read\|Description\|Error\|API)' -v -count=1` | 27/27 regex-matched tests pass (16 `resourcecore`, 11 `internal/bridge/resourcecore`); full unfiltered `go test ./resourcecore ./internal/bridge/resourcecore -count=1` runs and passes all 37 top-level test functions across both packages (23 + 14). |
| Boundary | `go test ./... -count=1` | Every package this change touches is green (`internal/bridge/resourcecore`, `resourcecore`, `internal/domain`, `internal/app/catalogo`, `internal/app/recursos`, `internal/postgres`, `internal/core`, `internal/config`, `migrations`). Two unrelated failures persist, both pre-existing and untracked, outside the 6B edit surface and outside this SDD change: `agent/skills/golang-cli/assets/examples` (skill scaffold missing example deps) and `internal/tui` (fails to build on untracked `internal/tui/suppliers_admin.go`, work-in-progress from an unrelated `proveedores-maestro-ux` branch, confirmed via `git status --porcelain` / `git ls-files`). |

## Delivery — stacked-to-main PR chain (7 PRs, Stage-level granularity)

Delivered as issue #139 + PRs #140–#146 (`feat/resource-master-core-1-domain` through `-7-sdd-delivery-record`), each stacked on the previous, split at Stage boundaries rather than the original 29 unit boundaries — the working tree had no intermediate per-unit commits, so true per-unit isolation would have required git archaeology; the user explicitly chose Stage-level granularity when asked.

Building each PR's isolated diff (rather than trusting that the already-`sdd-verify`d whole compiles as separate slices) surfaced three real, pre-existing issues that had never been caught before because CI had never run against any isolated slice of this code:

1. **`internal/domain/catalog_mutation.go`** — `attributeRulesFromRecords` built `AttributeRule` via a full struct literal when `CatalogRuleRecord` and `AttributeRule` share an identical field set; `golangci-lint`'s `staticcheck` (S1016) flagged it. Fixed to a direct struct conversion, `AttributeRule(record)`.
2. **`internal/postgres/catalog_admin_repository_v2_integration_test.go`** — `var repo domain.CatalogAdminRepositoryV2 = NewCatalogAdminRepositoryV2(nil)` carried a redundant explicit interface type annotation (`NewCatalogAdminRepositoryV2` already returns that exact interface type); `staticcheck` (ST1023) flagged it. Fixed to `repo := NewCatalogAdminRepositoryV2(nil)` — same runtime behavior, same interface-typed variable.
3. **Real cross-layer dependency in the original PR3/PR4 split**: `internal/postgres/catalog_admin_repository_v2_integration_test.go` and `internal/postgres/resource_lifecycle_integration_test.go` (both legitimate Stage 3H/3I canonical-reactivation-E2E and V2-conformance integration tests) call into `internal/app/{catalogo,recursos}` methods that don't exist until the application-layer PR. They — and the one small `runCatalogWriteTxV2` transaction helper in `catalog_admin_repository_v2.go` that only they exercised — moved from the PostgreSQL PR to the application-services PR so every PR in the chain is independently buildable, lintable, and testable in isolation (verified via `git checkout <branch>` + `go build`/`go vet`/`gofmt -l`/`golangci-lint run`/`go test` at every step, not just the final cumulative state).

None of these three findings contradict this document's own Stage 6 / P5 / P6 claims above — all were pre-existing in the already-`sdd-verify`d code, in files/lines this change's units did touch, and are now fixed and independently verified clean per PR.
